What this panel is for. Every number here was measured on 2026-09-07 by a command named beside it. It exists because the dashboard previously reported 5,105 bundle downloads against a target of 1,000 and read that as met, when those fetches are automated. Read this tab before any other.
Loop 32, 2026-09-09. Google indexed the first URL this project has ever had, and two money-path defects were found and fixed: hosted buyers were losing their data, and crawlers were having live Stripe sessions created for them.
MeasureReading
Google index1 of 141 URLs, the homepage, crawled 2026-09-08T17:20:24Z, from 0 of 126. 140 of 141 remain uncrawled and impressions are still zero. Googlebot made exactly one sitemap fetch in 24.7 hours.
Registry referralsflat, and exactly flat: every count byte-identical to the baseline, and zero registry-referred requests in the 24.7 hours since. four of the five changes had been live for 1.4 to 2.6 hours at window close, so this measures deployment, not effect. The recorded criterion says re-run on or after 2026-09-15.
Human page views, corrected137 off-homepage page views on a render-proof filter became 14 to 39 per week. one user-agent string touched 53 of 141 pages from eight countries, and 18 of 53 render-proven agents appeared from more than one country. A rendering crawler fetches assets too, so an asset fetch proves a browser engine, not a person.
Inbound links18 of 18 still live, including all four placed on 2026-09-08. The two lost earlier are still lost. One remains fragile because it was shipped by a live-mirror deploy against a repo that is behind live.

Money-path defects found and fixed. On the hosted endpoints the tenant IS the token, so a buyer who reconnected with their licence key landed on a different tenant and left their invoices behind. license_activate was a stub whose description even contradicted its own schema. It now binds a verified key to the caller's own anonymous token, using the same mechanism a hosted purchase already used. Verified live: free to pro on the same token with the invoice intact; a garbage key and a valid key for another product both change nothing.

A crawler sending a browser-shaped user agent with accept */* had a real cs_live_ checkout session created for it, while python-requests was correctly turned away. That put a Stripe call on the critical path of requests nobody would pay for, which is the likely cause of 691 gateway timeouts on /buy/* in 25 hours, every one with no recognisable browser, and it inflated the human-checkout-sessions figure. A navigation signal is now required of every request.

The zero-install hosted URL sat at character 20,676 of a 21,905-character product page while the page led with the price. It now sits at character 153 on all 30 hosted servers, driven by a list derived from the manifests behind a drift test that was control-tested.

A metric retired. the discovered/unknown coverage split is not trackable: two censuses 15 minutes apart disagreed on 82 of 141 URLs, so only in-index and ever-crawled are reportable

Loop 31, 2026-09-09. Found why none of these servers appears in VS Code's built-in MCP picker, and it is a property of our own registry entries rather than anything about the product.
Hypothesis for why VS Code's picker excludes usTested against its 252 entries

repository.subfolder0 of 252 gallery entries have repository.subfolder; 19 of 100 sampled registry entries do; all 89 of ours do

com.bestremotetools/office-suite-all-servers-one-install published pointing at a standalone repo with no subfolder, same namespace as a subfolder entry so only the tested variable differs Watch: scripts/vscode-gallery-watch.mjs.

Glama. The real surface, and it is already working: the official registry is mirrored into glama.ai/mcp/connectors with no account, and our presence went from 4 to 25 servers between 09-07 and 09-09 with nobody doing anything. Connectors carry the identical scoring rubric and re-score daily. license_activate was the minimum-scoring tool on 20 of 20 connectors at mean 2.26 and low 1.4, while license_status scored 4.12. It is registered ONCE in packages/mcp-license and inherited by all 31 servers, and 40 percent of the score is the minimum tool score, so one string capped the fleet. Rewritten to 202 characters, verified accurate against the code.

Docker. https://github.com/docker/mcp-registry/pull/5030 is open and locally validated through all four CI steps, but their queue has not merged anything since 2026-08-21 and carries 1,146 open pull requests, so it is a wait not a lever.

Found and fixed on the way. license_status at 534 characters and license_activate at 674 were both over the estate's own 220 ceiling at HEAD, registered once and inherited by all 31 servers, so the description contract test was failing on every server before this loop. SERVER_COUNT was 30 while 31 servers gate Pro, so every free-tier cap message and bundle link named a stale count. Fixed in both the licence package and the hosted shim. a browser asking for /mcp/<server> was handed raw JSON, fixed by content negotiation.

Corrected. an earlier draft this loop said real people were arriving from Google search: Search Console URL Inspection says those URLs are unknown to Google and have never been crawled, and several requests carry an HTML entity fused into the path, which no browser produces

Loop 30, 2026-09-08. The registry namespace lever was reported yesterday as needing a human. It did not. It is claimed, measured and live.

served /.well-known/mcp-registry-auth from a git-connected Cloudflare Pages site, no DNS token, no human step. Scope: registry SEARCH only. No client picker inherits the ASCII sort: VS Code, Goose, Dify, Smithery and Windsurf each re-rank by their own rule.

Also this loop. PR 13473 was closed for adding multiple servers. Four single-server PRs opened; 13963 is the first fully green entry this project has ever put in front of that maintainer, with a live Glama badge. three PRs open on block/goose, one server each, every endpoint tested as a stranger first. 4 new inbound links on claudhq.com, claudflow.com, bestremotetools.com, all four independently re-fetched and confirmed. Storefront at 141 URLs with 14 new reference pages and a privacy page.

Found elsewhere and not fixed: worthmyclaim.com lost wrongful-death-settlement-calculator and settlement-formula both 404, and sitemap.xml 404s. Cause: published to live but never committed; a later repo deploy rebuilt without them. Recoverable: no. Not in the repo, no local copy, no Wayback snapshot.

The metric that was wrong

Statistic across 597 release assetsValueWhat it means
Total recorded downloads5314Reported as installs against a target of 1,000
Mean / median per asset8.90 / 8Near-uniform
Coefficient of variation0.417Human demand is a power law; this is flat
Share held by the top 10 of 597 assets5.7%A power law would put this at 40-70%

Obsolete releases score the same per asset as the current one, and nothing in this repo downloads its own assets. This is external machine traffic. Full working: docs/AUDIENCE_REALITY_R1.md.

The three things a person must do

  1. Run npm login --auth-type=web, approve the browser tab, confirm npm whoami prints theluckystrike, then scripts/publish-all.sh --go. Until then npx -y @theluckystrike/mcp-<name> returns E404 for every visitor, and that command is printed on every product page and in every guide. Sixty seconds.
  2. Claim the Glama listing. It is the only remaining blocker on the awesome-mcp-servers pull request, which is the highest-traffic directory this project can reach for free.
  3. Decide on the namespace experiment. The registry sorts strict ASCII on the whole namespace string, so io.github.theluckystrike can never reach page one on a contested token. A namespace from a .com domain already on the operator's Cloudflare account would sort 10 to 45 places earlier. See the addendum in docs/SEO_INDEXATION_R1.md.

MCP Time Tracker @theluckystrike/mcp-time-tracker

Track billable time without leaving the chat.

What it does. Starts and stops timers, records manual entries, sets hourly rates per project, and turns the log into reports grouped by project, day, task or tag, CSV exports and invoice-ready line items. Money is computed from seconds and integer cents in one rounding step so totals always add up.

Who it is for. Freelancers, consultants and developers who already work inside Claude Code, Cursor or Claude Desktop and bill by the hour.

Example. "Start a timer for Acme, task: API refactor" ... "Stop it and show me this week's hours per project with amounts."

FreeUnlimited timers and entries; reports, lists and CSV cover the last 7 days; 2 projects with rates. Currency per entry (EUR, USD, GBP, PLN).Pro $19Full history, invoice_summary, group by tag, unlimited rated projects. BuyData stays~/.local/share/mcp-servers/time-tracker/ Installclaude mcp add time-tracker -- npx -y @theluckystrike/mcp-time-tracker Statusnpm missing registry published 0.20.0 (io.github.theluckystrike/time-tracker-timesheet-billable-hours) github published validation 24/24
ToolWhat it does
timer_startStart tracking time on a project. Only one timer runs at a time: starting a new one stops and logs the previous one.
timer_stopStop the running timer and log it as a time entry. Returns the duration and the entry id.
timer_statusShow the running timer, how long it has been running, and today's total so far.
entry_addLog time you already worked. Give start plus either end or minutes.
entry_listList logged time entries as a compact table. Free tier shows the last 7 days.
entry_deleteDelete one time entry by id.
entry_editChange fields of an existing entry. Only the fields you pass are changed.
project_set_rateSet the hourly rate used to turn tracked hours into money for a project.

MCP Deposits @theluckystrike/mcp-deposits

Security and retainer deposits, held per client, on the same engine as your invoices.

What it does. Records a security or retainer deposit when the money arrives, in minor units and in the currency it arrived in, and then answers for it: what is held per client and per currency, since when, and what has already gone. Applying a deposit to an invoice writes the payment onto that invoice under both locks, adding to what was already paid rather than replacing it, so a bank transfer that landed first is not erased; it can never pay out more than the deposit still holds or more than the invoice still owes, and a deposit is never converted between currencies. Refunds hand money back without touching the invoice, the stored status is derived from the movements every time one is written, and the statement is one client in one currency, as text or as an A4 PDF that matches the invoice and credit note next to it.

Who it is for. Anyone who takes money before the work: landlords and letting agents on security deposits, agencies and consultants on retainers, trades on up-front payments.

Example. "Record a 500 euro security deposit from Nordic Print, received today." ... "Apply 300 of that deposit to INV-2026-0001." ... "How much are we holding for Nordic Print?" ... "Refund the rest, paid back by bank transfer."

MCP Per Diem @theluckystrike/mcp-per-diem

Statutory travel allowances on the rate tables the tax authorities publish, and the trips you priced with them.

What it does. Prices one business trip against a bundled rate table and shows the arithmetic: the allowance per day, the partial-day fraction the scheme's own ladder produced, every deduction for a meal somebody else paid for, and the total in the scheme's own currency. Three schemes ship with it: the Polish delegation regulation at home and per country, the HMRC benchmark scale rates for travel inside the UK, and the US GSA CONUS standard M&IE with the lodging cap. Start and end are instants rather than wall clocks, so a trip across a clock change is 23 or 25 hours and the Polish 24-hour period and the US calendar day give different, correct answers to the same trip. Trips can be saved under a name with a TRIP-YYYY-NNNN id, listed with a total per currency, reported per scheme and per month, and handed to the expense tracker as ready-made expense_add arguments. The tables are files inside the package, not a feed, so the same trip priced twice gives the same answer, and every rate carries its authority, instrument, source URL and effective date.

Who it is for. Anyone who claims or pays a statutory travel allowance: Polish contractors and employers on delegacja and diety, UK sole traders and companies on the HMRC benchmark rates, and US federal or corporate travellers on the GSA standard.

Example. "What is the per diem for 58 hours in Krakow, leaving Tuesday 8am, with breakfast on the first day and two nights in a hotel?" ... "Same trip under the HMRC rates." ... "Save it as the Krakow audit trip." ... "Total my per diems this quarter per scheme."

FreeRate lookups and calculations are unlimited on every tier: the tables are public regulation, and metering the reading of a regulation would be charging for the tax authority's work. 5 trips saved per calendar month, counted by start date, and unlimited trip lists.Pro $19Unlimited trips saved, the expense-tracker export payloads (one per currency, ready for expense_add), and the report of totals per scheme and per calendar month. MCP Asset Register @theluckystrike/mcp-asset-register

A fixed asset register that depreciates on the rates the tax authorities actually publish, with the schedule, the journal and the gain on sale.

What it does. Keeps a register of what you bought, what it cost, when it went into use and which category it falls in, and returns the rate, the useful life, the convention, the full schedule year by year or month by month, one month's journal entry, and the gain or loss when you sell. Three tables ship with it: the Polish annual rates from the annex to the CIT and PIT acts keyed to the KST classification (33 rows, with declining-balance eligibility and the art. 16k switch to straight line), the UK capital allowance pools at 18 and 6 percent with the annual investment allowance, and the US MACRS GDS half-year percentages for 3, 5 and 7 year property exactly as IRS Pub 946 Table A-1 publishes them. The convention is the table's, not the caller's: Poland charges from the month AFTER the asset enters the register, so an asset in service on 15 March starts on 1 April and year one is nine twelfths, while the MACRS percentages already carry the half-year convention and a UK writing down allowance is not prorated by month at all. The same asset gives three different year-one figures under the three schemes and all three are right. Money is integer minor units end to end and the schedule is allocated by cumulative rounding, so the periods sum to cost less residual to the minor unit and the months sum to their year. The tables are files inside the package, not a feed, and every rate carries its authority, instrument, source URL and effective date.

Who it is for. Anyone who has to put a depreciation figure on a return or in a ledger: Polish companies and sole traders on the KST annex rates, UK businesses claiming capital allowances on the main and special rate pools, and US filers on MACRS GDS.

Example. "Add the Dell workstation, 8,499 zloty, in service 15 March 2026, computers." ... "Show me the schedule month by month." ... "Depreciate the same truck under declining balance." ... "What is April's journal entry?" ... "We sold the van for 12,000 in September, what is the gain?"

FreeDepreciation schedules are unlimited on every tier: the rates are public information a tax authority published, and metering the reading of a regulation would be charging for the government's work. The cap is on the SIZE of the register, 10 assets, plus unlimited asset_list and asset_dispose.Pro $19Unlimited assets in the register, the monthly journal entry with its ready-made expense_add payload (one per currency, no vat_rate because depreciation is a book charge), and the report of net book value by category and currency with the year's charge and every disposal in it. MCP Statement of Account @theluckystrike/mcp-statement-of-account

The one document that answers what a client actually owes you, aged as at any date, with the chaser drafted.

What it does. Reads the three books this suite already keeps -- the invoice ledger, the credit notes and the deposits -- and turns them into a statement of account for a period: the balance carried at the start, every invoice issued, every payment received, every credit note given, the deposits applied broken out of the receipts rather than added again, and the closing balance. The movements are in date order and the opening balance plus their sum equals the closing balance, so the document is auditable line by line. statement_aging splits what is still open into 0-30, 31-60, 61-90 and over 90 days past the due date AS AT the date asked for, which means an invoice issued after that date is not on the books, a payment made after it has not happened and a credit note issued after it has not been given. Due today is not overdue: day zero sits in a not-yet-due line reported beside the four buckets. paid_minor on the invoice is the authority and the payment rows are only the attribution, so a deposit application that raised paid_minor without writing a payment row still appears. It writes into no book it reports on: bytes and mtimes of the sibling stores are unchanged across all six tools, and the only file it owns is a register of the statements it built. Nothing here invents a late fee, an interest rate or a legal cost, and a dunning letter escalates in tone and never in figures.

Who it is for. Anyone who invoices and then has to chase: freelancers and small firms who need to send a client a statement rather than forwarding five invoices, and anyone who has to answer what was owed on a given date rather than what is owed now.

Example. "Send Acme a statement for June." ... "Who owes me money, aged." ... "What was outstanding on 10 June?" ... "Draft a firm chaser for Acme." ... "Show me every client, per currency, with the oldest overdue invoice."

Freestatement_aging is free and unlimited on every tier, for one client or for everyone: who owes me money is the question this server exists for, and a free tier that hides it is a demo. The meter is on the document that goes to a client, five distinct statements a calendar month, counted by client, period and currency, so rebuilding one already in the register is free forever. Plain-text statements and dunning at the friendly and firm levels are free.Pro $19Unlimited statements, the A4 PDF on the same page layout as your invoices with your logo and no footer credit, the level 3 final demand, and statements_report: every client at once, totalled per currency, ranked by what is overdue rather than by what is large, with the oldest overdue invoice in the whole book named. MCP Cash Book @theluckystrike/mcp-cash-book

One double-entry ledger derived from the books you already keep, proved to the minor unit.

What it does. Reads six sibling stores read-only -- the invoice ledger, the credit note and purchase order store, the deposit store, the expense ledger, the bank import and the fixed asset register -- and derives a debit and a credit for every movement in a period. Revenue and VAT output come off the invoices, receivables and the payments that clear them, deposits held as the liability they are, expenses by category with the VAT taken OUT of the gross rather than added on top, fixed assets entering service, and depreciation charged by month. The bank import posts nothing: a bank line and a payment record are one transaction seen twice, so cash is posted from the documents and each bank row is matched to a posted cash movement of the same amount, the same direction and a date within three days, as evidence written onto the line as bank_ref. What is left over is the output that matters, because a bank debit with no expense behind it is a payment nobody entered. Nothing is ever balanced with a plug: an entry whose own legs do not add up is posted exactly as the document states it and the difference is raised by name, with the entry, the source server and the source document behind every unit of it. Currencies are never added together, a purchase order is a memo that is never posted, a deposit applied to an invoice moves the liability rather than cash, and the ledger opens at nothing because an opening figure nobody can walk back to a document is the first invented number in a set of books. Every line carries the server, the document id and the ISO date it came from, and no sibling store changes bytes or mtime across any of the six tools.

Who it is for. Anyone running this suite who has to answer whether the books add up, and anyone who has to hand an accountant a ledger rather than six exports: freelancers and small firms at month end, and bookkeepers who need every figure to name the document it came from.

Example. "Build the ledger for June." ... "Does June balance?" ... "Show me every line hitting receivables." ... "Close June and tell me what is unposted." ... "Export the June ledger as CSV."

Freetrial_balance and ledger_lines are free and unlimited on every tier: whether the books add up is the question this server exists for, and a free tier that hides the answer is a demo. The meter is on the PERIOD, three distinct periods a calendar month, keyed by from, to and currency, so rebuilding one already in the register is free forever on every tier.Pro $19Unlimited periods, month_close with its trial balance snapshot and its list of what the month leaves unposted or inconsistent, ledger_export_csv as RFC 4180 text with no file written, and ledger_report with the movement and closing balance per account plus the purchase commitments held as a memo. MCP Amortization @theluckystrike/mcp-amortization

Loan and lease schedules from the terms of the agreement, to the minor unit.

What it does. Takes the terms of a credit agreement -- principal in minor units, the nominal annual rate in basis points, how often interest compounds, how often a payment falls due, the term in periods, annuity or straight principal, any arrangement fee, any balloon and the drawdown date -- and derives the level payment, the effective annual rate beside the nominal one, and the schedule period by period: opening balance, payment, interest, principal, closing balance, every figure an integer minor unit and every closing balance reaching the balloon, or zero, exactly. Compounding and payment frequency are two different clocks: the rate for one payment period is the equivalent rate taken through the compounding clock, (1 + r/m)^(m/p) - 1, never the nominal rate divided by the number of payments. The payment never varies and the last period absorbs the rounding residual in the interest and principal SPLIT rather than in the payment, because the payment is what the borrower is contractually due to pay and the split is not. A level payment rounded once can clear the debt before the declared term ends, and the schedule stops where the debt does rather than reporting periods the borrower does not owe. Early settlement and partial overpayment are costed gross AND net of the penalty, and a penalty larger than the interest saved is reported as a COST rather than a smaller saving. The journal debits interest expense and loan liability and credits cash in the cash book's own account ids, and only the interest reaches the expense payload, because booking the whole payment as an expense overstates the cost of the business by the principal every period and still reconciles perfectly against the bank. Nothing is posted from here, no schedule is stored, currencies are never added together, and month arithmetic clamps so a loan drawn on the 31st pays on the 28th in February.

Who it is for. Anyone who has to answer what a credit agreement actually costs before signing it, and anyone who has to book the payments afterwards: freelancers and small firms with equipment finance, a car lease or a working-capital loan, and the bookkeepers who have to split each payment between interest and principal.

Example. "Record the van finance: 40,000 EUR, 12 percent nominal compounded monthly, 12 monthly payments, drawn 15 January." ... "Show me the schedule." ... "What would settling at period 6 cost me?" ... "Give me the journal for March." ... "What do I owe across all of them at 30 June?"

Freeloan_schedule is free and unlimited on every tier: the payment and the interest are the question this server exists to answer, and a free tier that hides the answer is a demo. The meter is on the number of agreements held, three loans in the register, which is the unit of work.Pro $19Unlimited loans, loan_repay_early for settling or overpaying at any period costed gross and net of the penalty, loan_journal with its expense-tracker payload carrying the interest alone, and loans_report: what is owed per currency, when each next payment falls due, and the interest charged in a calendar year. MCP Work Order @theluckystrike/mcp-work-order

Job orders for trades and field work, priced the way the invoice will be.

What it does. Raises a work order against a client the invoice server already knows -- the same client record, not a second spelling of the name -- with a site address, the date it was asked for, what the job is and how urgent. Lines are logged as the job goes: labour as hours at an hourly rate, parts as a quantity at a unit cost in minor units with an optional markup percent. THE MARKUP GOES ON THE UNIT COST, NEVER ON THE LINE TOTAL, because the invoice server rounds a unit price into minor units first and computes the line from that stored value, so the marked-up unit is the only basis an invoice can reproduce: seven parts at 1299 with 15 percent is 1494 a unit and 10,458 on the line, where marking up the line total is 10,457, and the unit suite asserts that gap is exactly 1 so a change of basis fails the build rather than quietly re-pricing every job on the board. The status moves one step at a time, draft to scheduled to in_progress to done to invoiced, each step stamped with its date and a note; a skipped step is refused naming the step that IS next, because every step carries its own date and a job that went straight to invoiced was never marked done, so no completion report was produced and nothing records the day the work finished. Backwards is refused too: a job that has to go back is a new work order. No total is stored -- value, hours, materials and VAT are derived from the lines on every call, because a stored total is the copy that gets believed after somebody edits a line. Hours are counted on the LINE date, not on the order date, so a February call worked in March logs its hours in March. work_order_invoice_payload builds the invoice_create items and then runs the invoice server's own computeTotals over those items, so there is no second implementation to disagree, it posts nothing and marks nothing, and an order already invoiced refuses a second payload by name. An unknown client name with no address is refused, because a bare name that matches no client record is a misspelling far more often than a new customer. A labour rate is never improvised: there is no default rate in the shared profile today, so rate_minor is refused by name rather than invented onto a report the customer signs.

Who it is for. One-van trades and small field-service outfits: electricians, plumbers, heating engineers, appliance and equipment repair, groundworks, and anyone who takes a call, does the job, hands over a signed completion report and then has to raise the invoice without retyping a single figure.

Example. "Raise a work order for Harbour Cafe, 12 Quay Street, urgent, the walk-in is not holding temperature." ... "Three hours labour on the 8th at 85 an hour." ... "Seven thermostats at 12.99 each, 15 percent markup." ... "Mark it done on the 9th." ... "Completion report, then the invoice payload."

FreeFive OPEN work orders, which is a one-van trade, and 200 lines each on every tier. The cap counts open jobs (draft, scheduled, in progress) rather than jobs ever raised, so finishing one frees its slot, and work_order_delete on a draft with no lines is free on every tier because a way back that only a Pro key can reach is not a way back. The text completion report is free: handing the customer what was done is the thing the job was for. A byte-identical work order is refused BEFORE the cap is consulted, so the refusal names the id already stored rather than selling an upgrade, and burns neither a slot nor a WO number.Pro $19Unlimited open work orders, the A4 completion report PDF with the sign-off block, work_order_invoice_payload with VAT at the shared profile rate and rounding_drift_minor asserted to zero, and work_orders_report: the whole board by status, hours logged this month, and unbilled value per currency. MCP Catalogue @theluckystrike/mcp-catalogue

One price list and one rate card, kept where the invoice and the quote can both read them.

What it does. Holds a SKU as a code, a name, a unit, an optional VAT rate and price ROWS, each row a currency, a tier, a valid-from date and an amount in MINOR units; a role carries an hourly rate the same way. The price on a date is the latest valid_from at or before it, worked out on the call, so raising a price in July does not rewrite what June was quoted at, and sku_get names the row it picked, how many it considered and any later row already booked. THE STORE HOLDS MINOR UNITS BECAUSE THE TWO SERVERS THIS CATALOGUE FEEDS TAKE THE SAME PRICE IN DIFFERENT SCALES AND THE GAP IS EXACTLY 100x: invoice_create's item carries unit_price in MAJOR units (90 for 90 EUR) and quote_create's item carries unit_price_minor in MINOR units (9000 for 90.00 EUR), both are plain numbers, both are called the unit price, and neither tool can tell that the number it was handed was scaled for the other one, so 45000 passed as unit_price is a perfectly valid invoice line for EUR 45,000.00 that reconciles against itself. The unit suite measures the gap on the worked resolution: the correct payload nets 261,363 minor and the quote payload's field fed into the invoice engine nets 26,136,300, asserted as exactly 100x, and 1000x in a 3-decimal currency such as KWD. So lines_resolve builds BOTH payloads itself in one call with the scale printed against each, rather than returning the price and letting the caller pick the field, which would be wrong half the time and wrong by two orders of magnitude when it was. A date before every row has no price and that is a refusal naming the earliest row, because filling it with the earliest row reprices history: a job done in December 2024 would be billed at the January 2025 price and reconcile perfectly against a price list that did not exist yet. One row per currency, tier and valid-from date; setting that key again REPLACES the row and the response says what it was and what it became, because two rows on one key make the price that day a coin toss decided by array order. A price is never invented: no fallback, no profile default, no nearest match, and an unknown code or a code with no price in the currency and tier asked for is refused by name. No current price is stored, because a stored current price is the copy still being quoted a month after the rise. This server creates no invoice and no quote: lines_resolve returns arguments and says posted false.

Who it is for. Anyone who quotes and invoices the same catalogue twice: agencies and studios with a day rate and a handful of retained products, trades with a parts list and a labour card, and any small business whose prices are currently a spreadsheet somebody retypes into every quote.

Example. "Add WEB-AUDIT, a fixed price audit, 450 from the first of January." ... "We are putting it up to 495 from July." ... "What was it in March?" ... "Senior developer 85 an hour, junior 45." ... "Three audits, twelve months hosting, seven and a half hours senior and three and a quarter junior, priced as of the 15th of March, ready for an invoice."

Free25 SKUs, the one standard price tier, unlimited rate cards, and every text answer including lines_resolve and price_list_text. Withholding lines_resolve would withhold the one thing the sibling servers came here for, and a price list nobody can read is not a price list. sku_delete is free on every tier and the resolution register is what makes that safe: a code that has priced a line, or that a rate card points at, is refused by name with the times it was used and the last resolution id, because a code printed on a document somebody sent is a fact about that document. A byte-identical duplicate is refused BEFORE the free cap is consulted, and the adversarial suite asserts that refusal text does not contain "free tier": a duplicate is one product filed twice far more often than it is two products, and it burns neither a slot nor a place in the list.Pro $19An unlimited catalogue, price tiers beyond standard so trade and wholesale are a second column rather than a second catalogue, price_list_pdf as an A4 page, and catalogue_report: how many rows are in force today, which rows a later row already replaces, and which SKUs carry no price in the profile's default currency, which are the rows that stop a resolution dead. MCP Petty Cash @theluckystrike/mcp-petty-cash

A petty cash float on the imprest system, reconciled to the minor unit.

What it does. Opens a tin with an imprest amount and a custodian, records one voucher for every receipt paid out of it -- amount in whole minor units, date, category, description, payee and receipt reference -- and counts the cash whenever you ask, returning the expected balance, the difference to the minor unit and every voucher that count covers, all marked reconciled. A count is a FACT, so it moves the book balance: the difference is carried forward as an over or short rather than re-reported at every later count, which is what makes a tin that is short by a little every month visible as a run rather than as one number. The replenishment is imprest minus balance and never the sum of the vouchers: the two differ by exactly what the counts found over or short, and reimbursing the voucher total instead restores the float short by that difference every cycle while every reconciliation still reports clean. The difference comes back as its own cash_over_short journal line rather than folded into an expense category where it would look like postage. Under the imprest system the petty_cash account does not move at a replenishment at all: the journal credits cash and debits the expenses, per category, in the cash book's own account ids, so a category spelled three ways is one account and not three. A voucher larger than the balance on its own date is refused, and so is a back-dated one that would make any LATER day negative. A reconciled voucher cannot be deleted, because the cash it took out was counted. No balance is stored: every balance is derived on the call from the imprest, the top-ups, the vouchers and what each count found.

Who it is for. Anyone who holds cash for the small things and has to prove what happened to it: a one-tin office, a workshop, a site hut, a shop, a charity with a float, and the bookkeepers who have to turn a shoebox of receipts into a replenishment cheque and a journal that balances.

Example. "Open a EUR 500 office float, Anna holds it." ... "Stamps, 12.50, 2 March." ... "Taxi 34.80 on the 5th, receipt 4471." ... "I counted 297.95 in the tin on the 31st." ... "What does the replenishment cheque have to be, and what is the journal?"

Freereconcile is free and unlimited on every tier: whether the cash in the tin matches the paperwork is the question this server exists to answer, and a free tier that withholds the answer is a demo. voucher_delete and topup_record are free for the same reason -- a voucher typed in twice would otherwise cost a slot with no way back but a key. The meter is on the volume of record keeping: one float, and twenty vouchers a calendar month, which is a real one-tin office.Pro $19Unlimited floats and vouchers, replenish_request with the amount that restores the imprest, the vouchers it reimburses, the per-category expense_add-ready payload and the balanced double entry, and float_report with the balance against the imprest, what is unreconciled, the last count and the history of every difference a count has found. ToolWhat it does
float_openOpen a petty cash float on the imprest system and return its id. The imprest is the cash the tin is topped back up to at every replenishment, in whole minor units. Free tier: one float.
voucher_addRecord one voucher paid out of the tin and return its VOU-YYYY-NNNN number. More than the float holds is refused, and so is a byte-identical duplicate. Free tier: 20 vouchers a calendar month.
voucher_deleteDelete a voucher that was entered wrongly, by its VOU number. A voucher already covered by a reconciliation is refused: the cash it took out was counted, so removing it would make a past count wrong. Free.

replenish_requestWork out what puts the float back to its imprest: the amount, the vouchers it reimburses, the totals per category as an expense_add-ready payload, and the double entry. Writes nothing. Pro.
topup_recordRecord cash put into the tin: the amount in whole minor units, the date and where it came from. It reimburses every voucher up to that date, which is what a replenishment cheque does. Free.
float_reportReport every float: the balance against its imprest, what is unreconciled, the last count, and the history of what the counts found over or short, per float and in total. Pro.

README · build sprint · source

MCP Change Order @theluckystrike/mcp-change-order

Change orders against a quote or a work order, with the running contract value derived from what the client approved.

What it does. Raises a change order against a quote or a work order, kept the way a variation is kept on site: added, removed and changed lines, each with a quantity, a unit price in minor units, a reason and a date; a status that moves draft to sent, sent to approved or rejected, draft or sent to void, with every step dated and a step dated before the last one refused; and the running contract value as the original plus the deltas the client has APPROVED, with pending draft and sent deltas shown apart and never added in. The original contract value is stated ONCE per reference on the first change order and inherited by every later one, and a different figure is refused by name, because a contract with two original values has two running values and the customer sees whichever was typed last. No delta is stored: the delta, the VAT and the running value are derived from the lines on every call with the invoice server's own computeTotals, so there is no second implementation to disagree. A CHANGED LINE IS TWO ITEMS, NOT ONE, because one net item shows the customer nothing they can check: 3 x 450.00 becoming 5 x 420.00 is +750.00, and an item of quantity 1 at 750.00 reproduces from nothing on the change order the client signed, where a reversal of -3 x 450.00 and the revised 5 x 420.00 both reproduce on a calculator and sum to the same +750.00 because roundHalfUp is symmetric in sign. That symmetry is what lets a removal ride as a negative quantity through invoice_create, and it is what quote_create refuses, so the quote payload carries a ready flag instead of a promise. Once approved, the delta comes back as invoice_create-ready items in MAJOR units and quote_create-ready items in MINOR units in one call, the scale printed against each, and the unit suite feeds the MINOR figure into the invoice engine as though it were MAJOR and asserts the net is exactly 100x, re-derived from each payload's own items: on the worked change order 11,701,200 against the correct 117,012. Lines are added only while a change order is a draft; a sent one that needs another line is voided and raised again, so the client's approval always refers to what they were sent. A draft cannot be approved directly, because approval is the client's answer to something they were sent. Nothing is invented: a reference with no change order on file has no running value and says so rather than starting from nothing but its own delta. It reads exactly one file it does not own, the shared business profile, read-only and best-effort, and writes into no store but its own.

Who it is for. Agencies, studios and trades whose quoted jobs change after the quote: a second page added after the kickoff, hosting dropped, an audit widened, and then the argument three months later about what the contract is worth now and which of those changes the client actually agreed to.

Example. "Raise a change order against Q-2026-0003 for Harbour Cafe, original value 20,000: a second landing page, drop the hosting, widen the audit to five sites." ... "Add two landing pages at 450, remove twelve months of hosting at 39.99, and the audit was three at 450, now five at 420." ... "Sent on the 12th." ... "They approved it on the 15th, what is the contract worth now?" ... "Build the invoice payload for the approved delta."

FreeFive OPEN change orders, draft and sent, and 200 lines each on every tier. The cap counts the ones the client has not answered rather than the ones ever raised, so approving, rejecting or voiding one frees its slot, and change_order_delete on a draft with no lines is free on every tier, because a way back that only a Pro key can reach is not a way back. contract_value is free on every tier: the running value is the thing the change orders exist to answer, and withholding it would withhold the record. A byte-identical change order is refused BEFORE the cap is consulted, so the refusal names the id already stored rather than selling an upgrade, and burns neither a slot nor a CO number.Pro $19Unlimited open change orders, change_order_document as a plain-text document with every line, its reason, the contract value before and after and an approval block for the client to sign, and change_order_invoice_payload: the approved delta as invoice_create items in MAJOR units and quote_create items in MINOR units at once, with VAT at the shared profile rate and rounding_drift_minor asserted to zero. change_order_add_lineAdd one line to a draft change order: added or removed with a quantity and a unit price in minor units, or changed with the old and the new quantity and price, each with a reason and a date. Free.
change_order_statusMove a change order: draft to sent, sent to approved or rejected, draft or sent to void, stamping the date and a note. Approved, rejected and void are final, and a draft cannot be approved unsent. Free.

README · build sprint · source

MCP Delivery Schedule @theluckystrike/mcp-delivery-schedule

Dated deliverables against a quote or a work order, and what is late as at any date you name.

What it does. Opens a delivery schedule against a quote, a work order or a change order, and holds the dated deliverables on it: what is being handed over, the day it is due, its value in whole minor units when it is separately priced, and a status history that runs planned to in progress to delivered to accepted, each move carrying the day it actually happened and the client's acceptance note on the accepted one. LATE IS NOT A STATUS HERE, IT IS A READING: a stored late flag is a fact about the afternoon somebody last ran the report and goes on being reported long after the work lands, so nothing derived is stored and late, due today, not yet due, delivered on time and delivered late are all worked out on the call against the as_of the CALLER passes. The same deliverable due 2026-04-20 and delivered 2026-04-24 reads not yet due at 2026-04-19, late by two days at 2026-04-22 and delivered late by four days at 2026-05-01, from one unchanged store, and the unit suite asserts all three. A deliverable is late only once as_of is PAST its due date, so one due on as_of sits in due_today, which is the same rule the statement server ages an invoice by. Every comparison is between two YYYY-MM-DD strings and every day count is the difference of two UTC midnights, and a suite replays every call under four timezones fourteen hours apart and asserts the responses are byte-identical, so a laptop that travelled cannot move a client's deadline. Nothing may predate the reference document: its own date is stated once on the schedule, and a due date or a status move before it is refused, because nothing can be owed, or delivered, before the document that ordered it exists. Accepting something that was never delivered is refused by name, because acceptance is the client's answer to a handover and there would be no delivered date to bill from. A deliverable with no value is NOT worth zero: it is counted apart wherever a total is printed and excluded by name from the milestone payload, because a price this server invented would be printed on a document a customer pays from. Once milestones are signed off, milestone_payload returns them as invoice_create-ready items in MAJOR units and quote_create-ready items in MINOR units at once, with the scale printed against each, and the unit suite feeds the MINOR figure into the invoice engine as though it were MAJOR and asserts the net is exactly 100x. It reads exactly one file it does not own, the shared business profile, read-only and best-effort, and writes into no store but its own.

Who it is for. Agencies, studios, consultancies and trades who quote a job in phases and then have to say, months later and on a date somebody else chose, which deliverables slipped, by how many days, and which ones the client has actually signed off and can therefore be invoiced.

Example. "Open a delivery schedule against WO-2026-0011 for Harbour Cafe, dated 1 April: wireframes due the 10th at 900, page copy due the 20th at 479.88, the built site due 1 May at 2100, handover training on the 8th." ... "Wireframes went out on the 9th, signed off on the 12th." ... "What was late as at 22 April?" ... "And as at 5 May?" ... "Build the invoice for everything they have accepted."

FreeThree OPEN delivery schedules, and 200 deliverables on each on every tier. The cap counts the jobs that still owe something rather than the jobs ever run, so accepting the last deliverable completes a schedule and frees its slot without deleting the record, and delivery_schedule_delete on an empty one is free on every tier, because a way back that only a Pro key can reach is not a way back. late_report is free on every tier: what is late is the question the server exists for, and withholding it would withhold the one answer the client is going to ask for. A second schedule against a reference that already has one is refused BEFORE the cap is consulted, so the refusal names the id already stored rather than selling an upgrade.Pro $19Unlimited open schedules, delivery_schedule_document as a plain-text document with every deliverable, its due date, its status, the delivered and accepted dates, the counts as at a date and a sign-off block for the client, and milestone_payload: the delivered-and-accepted deliverables as invoice_create items in MAJOR units and quote_create items in MINOR units at once, with VAT at the shared profile rate, rounding_drift_minor asserted to zero, and anything left out listed by name under excluded rather than billed as zero. Reading the table

  • Measured beats estimated. Rows whose reach cell says MEASURED come from this estate's own numbers (X replies earned zero in 86% of 397 cases; LinkedIn buyer share 29-47%; estate clicks ~1,632/28d). Rows marked estimate are ranked below any measured row of similar size.
  • Registries compound, posts decay. A registry or marketplace entry keeps producing installs for months; a social post is a one-day spike. Every row above impact 60 is a registry, a marketplace, or a search surface.
  • The pipeline stops at the human steps. Four rows need one click each (npm login, Smithery, Glama, extensions directory). Each is under a minute and unlocks a terminal-only surface behind it.
  • Budget rule. After two weeks, keep only channels with at least one measured click to mcp.zovo.one or one install; drop the rest.

Invoice PDF

A4 single page, clean grid. Header (business name left, INVOICE + number right), issue/due/status block, BILL TO, 4-column line table with per-line tax, right-aligned Subtotal/Tax/Total with Total in EUR, payment details with IBAN and reference, free-tier footer. Nothing cut off, nothing overlapping, totals readable at 80 dpi. Two cosmetic gaps: line amounts carry no currency symbol (only the Total does), and BILL TO shows only the client name because invoice_create auto-created Acme with no address.

Free tier in a first session

  • spreadsheet: 200-row write cap at sheet_add_column on a 400-row sheet (high). Message is clear but the tool still reports success and writes a file that looks complete. Data loss risk.
  • price-tracker: alerts_pending is Pro-only at "whether anything dropped" (high). Blocks the core promise of a price tracker on free.
  • invoice: overdue_report is Pro-only at "which invoices are overdue" (medium). invoice_list covers it, so the free user still gets an answer.

What an MCP product owner should measure, in funnel order: discovered, installed, first call, value delivered, reliable, paid. Every indicator names its instrument so it can be re-collected with node scripts/kpi.mjs. Collected 2026-09-07T02:16:00.387Z. Status: met = at or past target; progress = non-zero and below target; zero = instrument works and reads zero; unmeasured = instrument not yet wired.

License keys minted0 keys5zerowrangler kv key list --remote on LICENSESOne key per paid session; zero means no purchase reached /success.
Upgrade link clicks (humans, 7d)304 clicks20metGET https://mcp.zovo.one/stats/clicks, sum of by_src[*].last7d. WARNING: the worker guard is start-anchored, so any browser User-Agent is counted, including this project's own agents. docs/CONVERSION_R1.md measured 77.9% of clicks carrying a src no live page emits. Treat as an upper bound, never as human demand.The click is the step between a cap message and a checkout session; without it a stalled funnel and a message nobody reads look the same.
Click to checkout-session ratio12.5 % (sessions per 100 clicks)40progress100 * checkout sessions from humans (last 100, Stripe) / upgrade link clicks (7d, /stats/clicks) - the two windows differ (last 100 ever vs last 7 days), so this is an approximation, not the true window-matched ratioSeparates whether people are seeing the link (clicks) from whether the checkout page itself converts (ratio); a link with clicks and no sessions points at the redirect or the Stripe page, not the message.
Pro tenants on hosted endpoints0 tenants20zeroStripe-bound tenants (REMOTE_DATA bind: prefix, 0) plus distinct lic: tenant ids whose key the billing worker minted (LICENSES session:*, 0 keys). EXCLUDED: the 2652 raw lic: documents are per (key, server) pairs, not tenants (249 distinct ids), and 249 of those ids are validation probes signed locally by scripts/sign-license.mjs, never bought.Real Pro usage on hosted. The old count read every lic: document, so one validation run over 16 servers looked like 16 paying tenants.

Reading the KPIs as a specialist

  • Hosted activation is the leading indicator. Registries and directories only count hosted use, and an anonymous token is the cheapest possible first call. Watch anon tokens and tenant documents before downloads.
  • Tool reach beats tool count. A server that is correct on direct probes but loses to a built-in reader delivers nothing; the first-prompt reach rate is the number to protect, and descriptions are its lever.
  • Probes contaminate funnel metrics. Validation runs create checkout sessions and Pro tenants; since 2026-09-03 they are tagged (metadata.probe, signed keys) and excluded. Pre-tag sessions are excluded by timestamp.
  • Latency has a cliff, not a curve. Round 4 measured first-prompt misses when connect plus tools/list ran past about a second; p50 is tracked against 800 ms.
  • Until paid sessions are non-zero, every other number is a proxy. The KPI that closes the loop is paid sessions and minted keys; the rest exist to explain why it is still zero.

Organic score = 100 x listed x findable x reach. listed: 1 live, 0.5 PR/submission pending, 0.3-0.2 blocked on a human/CI step, 0 skipped/not published. findable for registry-backed surfaces is MEASURED via fully-paginated /v0/servers?search=<token> calls (limit=100, cursor loop -- fixes a prior single-page undercount risk): p(seen|query) = 0 if our current-version row for THAT server does not appear, else min(1, 10/rank), using the row's own best rank restricted to that server's registry name(s) (a token like 'price' that also substring-matches a different server's long slug, e.g. office-suite, is NOT credited to the wrong server). findable = mean of p over 56 natural query slots across all nine servers' query_set (was 41 across six servers before currency/docx/timezone existed). Per-server organic = 100 x mean(p) over that server's own query set. 'Hosted endpoints' findable = share of the 56 query slots whose matched row belongs to a server that now carries a non-empty remotes[] (8 of 9 servers: everything except office-suite). fleet.fleet_score is a noisy-OR over the nine per-server organics (100 x (1 - product(1 - p_i/100))), applied identically before and after for comparability.
